FILE: AUDIT_AND_ASTROLOGICAL_CORRECTNESS_1.0.4.md
================================================================================
# Jyotish Lab 1.0.4 — adversarial audit and astrological correctness report

## Scope and method

This audit reviewed the 1.0.3 project archive, source code, existing tests, calculation formulas, event/interpretation corpora, custom-period API paths and UI request wiring. It combines deterministic unit/regression tests, adversarial schema inputs, boundary tests and a manual traditional-method review. It is not a claim that astrological interpretation has been scientifically validated.

## Bugs found and corrected in this build

1. **D30 (Trimshamsha) even-sign boundary mapping.** The Cancer/Taurus/etc. even-sign bands used 5°, 12°, 20°, 25°, 30° instead of the commonly used Parashari 5°, 10°, 18°, 25°, 30° thresholds. This changed placements for portions of the 5°–25° range. The boundaries are corrected and now have tests immediately below/at each transition.
2. **False-positive Sunapha/Anapha Yoga.** The detector could treat the Sun alone in the 2nd/12th sign from the Moon as satisfying a yoga whose rule excludes the Sun. The detector now checks for at least one qualifying non-Sun graha after exclusion.
3. **Impossible dates and clock times accepted by request schemas.** `2001-02-30` and `25:99` passed regex-only validation. Calendar dates and 24-hour times are now parsed and rejected when invalid.
4. **Reversed custom date windows accepted at the API boundary.** Personal and mundane request models now reject reversed ranges and incomplete start/end pairs. Same-day windows are allowed (the forecast engine uses the full local day).
5. **Personal chat silently ignored custom forecast dates.** The cached chart builder omitted `forecast_start_date`/`forecast_end_date`, and its cache key therefore did not distinguish custom periods. Both values are now passed to the calculation and included in the cache key.
6. **Slow corpus fallback lookup and repeated counting.** Missing corpus keys previously scanned the full 135k-key index repeatedly, and rule counts were recomputed over the full corpus for each narrative. A prefix index and cached type counts now avoid those repeated full scans.
7. **Misleading strength labels.** The Shadbala calculation is a simplified six-component screening estimate, not a full canonical Shadbala implementation. Its method/note now disclose this. The Ashtakavarga BAV/SAV contributor tables remain available, but the sign-level shodhana/pinda reduction is explicitly labelled provisional rather than a canonical graha-wise shodhana result.

## Adversarial tests added

- Invalid calendar date and impossible time.
- Reversed and invalid custom forecast windows.
- Same-day country forecast window.
- Incomplete custom-leader payload.
- D30 threshold edges at 5°, 10°, 18° and 25°.
- Sunapha/Anapha exclusion of the Sun.
- Personal chat chart-cache propagation of custom dates.

Targeted run at build time: **21 passed** across adversarial validation, UI sections, custom forecast windows and country/leader synthesis.

## Astrological/technical correctness findings

### Astronomical layer

- Swiss Ephemeris is used for sidereal planetary positions with Lahiri ayanamsha; whole-sign houses are then calculated from the sidereal ascendant. This is a coherent declared convention, but house systems/ayanamsha choices must remain explicit because other software may use different settings. See the [Swiss Ephemeris programmer documentation](https://www.astro.com/swisseph/swephprg.htm).
- D1/D9 regression fixture remains: 6 Feb 2001, 17:57 IST, supplied coordinates 28.8777942 N / 74.0633619 E, Lahiri; Saturn is in Capricorn in D9, with Capricorn D9 Lagna and Saturn in D9 house 1.
- Unknown or rounded birth times remain a major source of uncertainty for Lagna, houses and Vargas. Astro-Databank describes source-based Rodden ratings and cautions that a rating is about provenance/reliability, not an absolute guarantee. See [Rodden rating guidance](https://www.astro.com/astro-databank/Help:RR).

### Traditional interpretation layer

- **Vimshottari Dasha:** recursive subperiod sequence starts from the parent lord; it is a coherent implementation of the 120-year cycle, but exact day counts depend on year-length convention and date/time handling. It should be cross-checked against a named reference implementation before claiming universal parity.
- **Vargas:** the app calculates 16 named charts, but not every divisional mapping is universally agreed across schools. D30 was corrected here; D40/D45/D60 and several higher Vargas still require cross-validation against a named, versioned reference dataset. D60 is especially sensitive to birth-time uncertainty.
- **Yogas/Doshas:** the detector is a rule-screening catalogue, not an exhaustive classical adjudicator. Many yogas need cancellation rules, benefic/malefic condition, combustion/war/strength, house-lord context and school-specific exceptions. A detected name should be interpreted as “screen-positive” unless all required conditions are implemented. The audit fixed one known exclusion error but does not certify every yoga definition.
- **Shadbala:** the six headings are present, but the components are approximations. The UI/report now disclose that fact rather than implying canonical strength scoring.
- **Ashtakavarga:** the seven BAV contributor tables are present. The separate legacy `ashtakavarga` output is an approximation, and the reduction/pinda path in `classical_ashtakavarga` is not yet a fully canonical graha-wise implementation. Do not present the reduction results as reference-equivalent until cross-validated.
- **Day/night dependent calculations:** some day/night choices use clock hour (before 06:00 or from 18:00) instead of computed local sunrise/sunset. This is not adequate for high-latitude or seasonally extreme cases; sunrise/sunset should be used in a future calculation-layer update.
- **Planetary aspects and combustion:** aspect/orb rules are simplified and fixed-orb; classical schools can differ. Combustion is not a universal binary switch and can depend on planet, motion and school conventions.
- **Jaimini/Tajaka/specialty layers:** the UI can offer multi-school synthesis, but this is not equivalent to a complete implementation of all Chara Dasha, Argalā, Jaimini aspects, Tajaka annual techniques, Prashna, KP sublords, Nadi, Sahams and other specialist methods.

### Interpretation corpus

- `interpretation_corpus.json` has 135,303 materialized entries (~82.7 MB). Runtime metadata multiplies this by 720 and can therefore display 97,418,160 virtualized combinations; the older test only asserted a lower bound of 853,200. Neither figure represents independently researched or astrologically validated rules. Many entries are controlled paraphrases over a finite rule skeleton, and exact-text deduplication does not remove semantic near-duplicates.
- `mundane_event_combinations.json` has 34,996 entries, but only 58 distinct text templates and repeated combinations per event class. The combinatorial count must not be described as that many independently sourced interpretations.
- Historical event rows frequently include date-only/noon proxies, as documented in `app/mundane.py`. Such records can support broad slow-planet analogue exploration, but they are not precise event charts and can inflate apparent matches when event definitions or windows are broad.

## Remaining priority backlog

### P0 — before calling the app astrologically reference-accurate

1. Cross-check planetary positions, Ascendant, D1, all 16 Vargas, Vimshottari MD/AD/PD, Shadbala and Ashtakavarga against a pinned reference dataset from a named software/version under the same Lahiri and calculation conventions.
2. Implement complete canonical Shadbala subcomponents or rename all dependent outputs as approximate screening metrics consistently in UI/PDF/API.
3. Implement BAV-wise Trikona Shodhana, Ekadhipatya Shodhana and pinda calculations against a verified worked example/reference, with tests for exceptions.
4. Compute sunrise/sunset for day/night-sensitive calculations and add DST/historical timezone regression cases.
5. Build a yoga rule registry with sources, prerequisites, exclusions, cancellation rules, school tags and confidence labels; test every rule with positive and negative fixtures.
6. Audit all divisional mapping boundaries (D2/D3/D4/D7/D9/D10/D12/D16/D20/D24/D27/D30/D40/D45/D60), including exact boundary values and random positions against a reference implementation.

### P1 — forecast quality and data governance

1. Evaluate event forecasts on a pre-registered, chronological holdout with one-to-one event matching, base-rate controls, precision, recall, false-positive rate, lead-time and abstention metrics. Keep the threshold frozen on blind data.
2. Add source URL, source date, birth-data reliability grade, event-time precision and uncertainty metadata to every reference profile/event. Quarantine uncertain times from Lagna/Varga validation.
3. Distinguish a *calculated astronomical event* (eclipse/ingress) from an *astrological interpretation* and from a *real-world event forecast* in API and UI.
4. Do not describe market, conflict, leadership, legal or health event classes as verified predictions absent independent prospective evidence.
5. Replace broad catch-and-continue paths with structured warnings where they can silently omit sections; add payload-size and latency tests for long horizons and large corpora.

### P2 — completeness

- Add school-specific configurations for Parashari, Jaimini, Tajaka/Varshaphala and optional KP rather than presenting a single blended engine as complete coverage.
- Add full Chara Dasha variants, Argalā, Jaimini aspects, annual-chart yogas, Sahams and relevant dasha systems only with reference fixtures.
- Add reproducible chart snapshots with engine version, ephemeris version, timezone database version, ayanamsha, house convention and calculation settings.
- Add randomized/property-based tests for longitude normalization, sign/house consistency, period ordering, input bounds, serialization and output determinism. Hypothesis is an appropriate tool for this; see [Hypothesis documentation](https://hypothesis.readthedocs.io/en/latest/).

## Empirical calibration boundary

Astronomical calculations can be checked against ephemerides and other software. Astrological meanings and predictions require a different kind of evaluation. Controlled tests have been published, including Carlson's 1985 double-blind study in *Nature* ([paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/318419a0)); that literature is contested, so it should be represented fairly, not treated as proof for or against every Jyotish method. This build validates code invariants and fixes identified rule bugs; it does **not** establish that astrological event forecasts are empirically reliable.

## Deployment gate

1. Install dependencies from `requirements.txt` in a clean environment.
2. Run `python -m py_compile app/*.py` and `node --check static/app.js`.
3. Run targeted tests: `pytest -q tests/test_adversarial_validation.py tests/test_ui_sections.py tests/test_custom_forecast_windows.py tests/test_leader_country_synthesis.py`.
4. Run the full suite with an explicit timeout and collect per-test durations; do not treat timeout as a pass.
5. Smoke-test `/api/health`, personal analyze/chat with and without custom ranges, country forecast with same-day/reversed ranges, leader chart overlay, PDF export, and D9 fixture.
6. Compare a fixed set of natal charts and boundary positions against the pinned reference calculator before production release.
7. Deploy to staging, preserve previous build and data files, validate logs and payload size, then promote. Roll back the archive if D1/D9/D30 or date-window smoke tests regress.

## Final test-run status

- Python compilation: passed.
- JavaScript syntax check: passed.
- Adversarial/schema/UI/custom-period/leader-synthesis targeted group: **20 passed**.
- Additional D9 regression, Varga assignment, long-horizon prediction, yoga interpretation and corpus regression group: passed in targeted runs (19-test group: **19 passed**).
- Full combined suite: reached **57 passing tests before the execution timeout**, without reporting an assertion failure. The combined process did not finish; this is not recorded as a full-suite pass. The remaining high-cost tests passed in targeted subsets, but a complete CI run in a less constrained environment is still a release gate.
- Live health endpoint smoke test: `/health` returns `status=ok` and version `1.0.4`.

## Current conclusion

This release fixes confirmed code defects and makes several approximation boundaries explicit. The most important remaining issue is not missing interpretation volume; it is reference-equivalent calculation coverage and blind empirical forecast evaluation. The next work should prioritize correctness fixtures and canonical subcomponent calculations rather than increasing the nominal number of corpus combinations.

# Changelog

## 1.0.2 — Custom forecast windows and deeper political/personal synthesis
- Added optional exact start/end date ranges for both country mundane forecasts and personal forecasts.
- Added public uprising, government collapse, abrupt leadership removal and constitutional-crisis event classes.
- Added three-layer event interpretations and removed exact duplicate corpus rows without deleting unique combinations.

# Jyotish Lab 1.0.0 — historical mundane validation

- Reworked retrospective mundane testing into a chronological development/holdout replay.
- Forecasts are issued from historical anchors with a fixed 365-day horizon and annual anchor cadence for the India/Israel validation fixtures.
- Threshold selection is performed only on the development period and frozen for the blind holdout.
- Predictions are collapsed to one prediction per event class per forecast year and actual events are one-to-one matched, preventing overlapping windows from inflating hit counts.
- Added cached as-of-date historical signatures so replay does not repeatedly recompute the same event charts.
- Added `scripts_run_validation.py` and `data/mundane_backtest_report.json` as internal regression artifacts. No India/Israel UI controls were added.
- The validation report deliberately does not claim 80% accuracy where the blind holdout does not support it.

## 1.0.3 — Country + leader synthesis
- Added optional selected or custom leader birth data to country forecasts.
- Leader timing uses the same custom forecast period as the country chart.
- National and leader charts remain separate evidence layers; date overlaps are shown as concordance signals without an uncalibrated combined probability.
- Unknown birth times are labelled and use noon only as a reference time.
- Added personal chat handling for court/litigation and sibling/sister/brother questions with appropriate chart-layer limitations.
- Added `LEADER_COUNTRY_SYNTHESIS_AND_DEPLOYMENT.md` with methodology, data-quality policy, validation and deployment steps.

## 1.0.4 — adversarial audit and calculation transparency
- Corrected even-sign D30 Trimshamsha boundaries (5°/10°/18°/25°/30°).
- Fixed Sunapha/Anapha false positives when the Sun was the only planet in the relevant sign from the Moon.
- Added real calendar-date, 24-hour time, timezone and custom-window validation to request schemas.
- Passed personal custom forecast dates through the deterministic chat chart cache and included them in its cache key.
- Clarified that Shadbala component totals and Ashtakavarga reductions are screening approximations where the implementation is not canonical.
- Added `AUDIT_AND_ASTROLOGICAL_CORRECTNESS_1.0.4.md` with research sources, confirmed fixes, limitations and a prioritized correctness backlog.

## 1.0.5 — Calculation validation and personalized Muhurta
- Added activity-specific personalized Muhurta search for puja, job joining, property purchase/sale, business opening, major expense/payment, property registration/legal signing, travel, and education.
- Added location-aware local sunrise/sunset, Lahiri Panchanga, Tithi, Nakshatra/Pada, Nitya Yoga, Karana, weekday Rahu Kalam/Yamaganda/Gulika screens, and optional natal Moon Tara Bala/Chandra Bala.
- Added API endpoint `/api/muhurta/search`, UI controls for activity/date range/location, explainable reasons/conflicts, 90-day range limit, and explicit rule-score limitations.
- Added exploratory Muhurta tests and documentation for reference validation, deployment, and known gaps.
- Shadbala and Ashtakavarga limitations remain explicitly documented rather than being represented as complete canonical calculations.

- Corrected a major Ashtakavarga bug: the target sign was held fixed across all twelve BAV output signs; now the target sign varies correctly, standard BAV totals sum to 337, and graha-wise shodhana/pinda are exposed.
- Updated the full Mundane validation report after the chronological blind replay: Israel holdout precision 33.3% (3 hits/9 predictions); India holdout precision 5.0% (1 hit/20 predictions). Neither reaches the requested 80% precision target.


## 1.0.6 — Security audit, hardening and UI reliability
- Added a 1 MB request-body cap, security response headers and a restrictive CSP; removed inline HTML event handlers and moved action dispatch to one delegated handler.
- Disabled world-event sync unless a server-side `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN` is configured; compare tokens in constant time and stopped returning subprocess output to clients.
- Redacted birth and location details from application/engine logs, removed internal cache metrics from public health output, and replaced internal exception details with generic API errors.
- Added bounded library/chat/backtest inputs and forecast horizon/date-range validation; constrained geocoder query/response sizes.
- Required HTTPS for remote AI provider endpoints, allowed plain HTTP only for localhost development, capped provider responses and sanitized provider errors.
- Escaped dynamic text before ReportLab Paragraph parsing to prevent user/AI-provided strings from being interpreted as PDF markup.
- Removed duplicate tab click wiring, shared the initial reference-library fetch, debounced/aborted library search requests, and implemented the previously missing mundane PDF export handler.
- Added security and UI wiring regression tests.

FILE: LEADER_COUNTRY_SYNTHESIS_AND_DEPLOYMENT.md
================================================================================
# Country + Leader Synthesis, Personal Scenario Coverage, and Deployment Plan

## What this release adds

- Optional country-leader overlay in the Mundane workspace. The national chart remains the primary collective chart; the leader chart is a separate corroboration/timing lens.
- Leader may be selected from the existing public-figure reference library or entered manually (name, date, time, birthplace, coordinates, timezone and time-quality label).
- If the selected leader has no reliable birth time, the app uses a labelled noon reference. It does not present that as a verified timed chart. House-based, Lagna-dependent and divisional conclusions are explicitly downgraded when time is unknown.
- Country forecast period is reused for the leader chart when the user selects a custom start/end range. The response reports date overlaps as research signals rather than inventing a single combined probability.
- Personal chat intent coverage includes court/litigation questions and sister/brother/sibling questions. These are framed as indirect indications in the native chart; an individual relative's own chart and real circumstances are distinct evidence.

## Methodological research notes

The implementation uses a layered, non-collapsing design:

1. **National chart:** canonical country/founding chart and its national-house framework.
2. **Leader natal chart:** a separate individual chart; it is not a proxy for the country or population.
3. **Timing:** the same user-defined forecast interval is applied to both chart engines where available.
4. **Concordance:** overlaps are listed with date, country event class and activation metadata. They are not called a validated probability, and no absence of overlap is treated as proof of weakness.
5. **Birth-data provenance:** selected profile source and time-status are displayed. If the time is unknown, noon is a computational reference only.
6. **Personal life domains:** career/business use existing D10 and 6th/10th/11th-house layers; spouse/relationship uses existing D9 and 7th-house layers; court/dispute questions direct the reader toward 6th/8th/9th/12th factors and D6/D30 when available; sibling questions use D3 and 3rd/11th-house factors where generated. No single placement or Yoga/Dosha is treated as sufficient by itself.

### Calibration rules

- Keep exact interpretation deduplication separate from genuine combinations. A rule's source, key and factor requirements must remain stable.
- Birth time confidence is a provenance field, not a score of astrological accuracy. Avoid precise Lagna/Varga claims where the time is not verified.
- Do not convert the leader's individual chart strength into a deterministic claim about government strength, policy competence, war result or public support.
- For future predictive validation, freeze event taxonomy and thresholds before the holdout. Report precision, recall, false-positive rate, class-level confusion matrix and the dates actually forecast. Do not tune on the blind holdout.
- Political/territorial/war event classes remain conditional astrological scenarios; they are not factual assertions or deterministic geopolitical forecasts.

## Data-quality policy

Public-figure profiles include `source` and `time_status`. For new profiles, capture source URL/title, access date, whether date and time are independently sourced, time-zone conversion, coordinates provenance, and a confidence class. Never fabricate a birth time. Unknown/approximate times should not be silently promoted to verified. For leader chart interpretation, report the uncertainty before discussing house or divisional-chart details.

## Deployment plan

### Local / staging

1. Back up the existing application directory and data folder.
2. Unzip this release into a fresh directory; do not overwrite a live directory in place.
3. Create a virtual environment and install `requirements.txt`.
4. Copy `.env.example` to `.env` only if needed; keep secrets outside source control.
5. Run `python -m compileall app`, `node --check static/app.js`, and `pytest -q`.
6. Start with `uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000` and verify `/`, `/api/library`, and a country request without a leader, with a database leader, and with custom leader details.
7. Verify custom period start/end are identical in the country and leader overlay, and that unknown-time leaders are visibly labelled.

### Production

1. Deploy to a staging environment first and retain the prior ZIP as a rollback artifact.
2. Configure process supervision (systemd, Docker Compose, or platform equivalent), TLS termination, request-size/time limits and log rotation.
3. Keep profile provenance and user-entered birth data out of public logs. Do not expose raw custom details in analytics.
4. Smoke-test country-only mode, saved leader mode, custom leader mode, invalid/missing coordinates, invalid timezone, unknown time, and custom date window.
5. Promote only after full regression tests pass; monitor exceptions and slow calculations. Roll back to the previous release if the new endpoint causes repeated 4xx/5xx or unacceptable latency.
6. Schedule a separate historical blind-validation run before making any accuracy claims.

## Known limitations

- The leader overlay is a first calibrated integration layer, not a validated model of government effectiveness.
- The current concordance display is date-overlap based; it does not calculate an empirically trained joint probability.
- Some reference profiles have unknown birth times and are only suitable for date-based planetary context, not reliable house/Varga analysis.
- The full test suite must be run in the deployment environment; targeted syntax and regression checks are not equivalent to full acceptance testing.

## Research references

- Astro-Databank, “Help: RR” — definitions of Rodden data ratings and explicit warnings about unknown/uncertain birth times: https://www.astro.com/astro-databank/Help:RR
- Astro-Databank overview — birth data source notes and rating system: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astrodatabank
- National chart technique overview (practitioner source; useful for documenting the tradition, not evidence of predictive validity): https://kerykeion.net/content/learn-astrology/mundane-national-charts
- Mundane/national chart practice overview and founding-moment uncertainty (practitioner source): https://astroleg.com/the-astrology-of-nations-how-countries-reflect-cosmic-archetypes/

These references describe astrological practice and birth-data provenance. They do not establish that astrology can reliably predict political outcomes.

FILE: MUHURTA_AND_VALIDATION_METHOD.md
================================================================================
# Jyotish Lab 1.0.5 — calculation validation and personalized Muhurta

## Scope

This release implements a personalized, date-range-based electional timing screen and extends the calculation-validation harness. It does **not** claim that astrological readings have scientifically demonstrated predictive accuracy. “Precision” here means numerical/timezone correctness, deterministic behavior, explicit rule provenance, and agreement with a declared tradition/reference where a fixture exists.

## Research basis and conventions

- Swiss Ephemeris programmer documentation: https://www.astro.com/swisseph/swephprg.htm
- Swiss Ephemeris sidereal/ayanamsha documentation: https://www.astro.com/swisseph/swisseph.htm
- PyJHora reference project and test notes: https://github.com/naturalstupid/PyJHora
- PyJHora Ashtakavarga implementation notes: https://github.com/naturalstupid/PyJHora/blob/main/src/jhora/horoscope/chart/ashtakavarga.py
- Shadbala components and unit conventions overview: https://parasara.net/calculators/shadbala
- Birth-time provenance / Rodden Rating: https://www.astro.com/astro-databank/Help:RR

These references serve different purposes. Swiss Ephemeris validates astronomical positions/time handling; PyJHora is a useful independent Jyotish reference implementation whose method settings must be matched; astrological tradition sources document rule conventions, not empirical proof that forecasts cause or reliably predict outcomes.

## Calculation validation strategy

1. Keep Lahiri sidereal mode explicit before sidereal calculations; do not depend on Swiss Ephemeris' default ayanamsha.
2. Use timezone-aware local civil time and convert to UTC before Julian-day calculation.
3. Validate real Gregorian dates, 24-hour times, IANA timezone names, coordinate ranges, and ordered/limited search intervals.
4. Preserve the established D9 regression fixture (2001-02-06 17:57 Asia/Kolkata; Capricorn D9 ascendant and Saturn in Capricorn / D9 house 1).
5. Exercise divisional-chart boundary cases immediately below/on/above boundaries; D30 uses unequal classical bands and sign-parity-specific mapping.
6. Check yoga exclusion logic and custom-date propagation/caching.
7. Muhurta smoke tests check local sunrise/set date, 8-fold weekday segment tables, valid Panchanga ranges, deterministic output, optional natal-Moon personalization, and range/activity validation.
8. Forecast backtests must be chronological, avoid future-event leakage, use one-to-one event matching, and report precision, recall, false positives and coverage separately. Do not optimize on the blind holdout or represent event-corpus coverage as predictive accuracy.

## Personalized Muhurta feature

Endpoint: `POST /api/muhurta/search`

Supported activity profiles: puja/spiritual practice, job joining, property purchase, property sale, business opening, major expense/payment, property registration/legal signing, travel start, and beginning study/exam preparation.

Request fields: `activity`, `start_date`, `end_date`, `latitude`, `longitude`, `timezone`; optional `natal_dob`, `natal_tob`, and `natal_timezone` enable Tara Bala and Chandra Bala screens. The search period is limited to 90 days. The current UI defaults to Kolkata and allows coordinates/timezone to be edited; users must choose the actual event location.

Each candidate includes local start/end, score, Tithi/Paksha, Nakshatra/Pada, Nitya Yoga, Karana, sunrise/sunset, supporting rules and conflicts. Daylight Rahu Kalam, Yamaganda and Gulika segments are screened using weekday-specific eight-part tables. The displayed score is a weighted heuristic score from an explicit rule screen, **not a probability** and not a universal classical ranking. Regional panchanga conventions and activity-specific rules differ; the app avoids claiming a universal muhurta.

Personalization currently uses the natal Moon longitude for Tara Bala and Chandra Bala. It does not yet calculate a complete event-specific Muhurta Lagna, full Panchaka, regional Masa/Amanta-Purnimanta constraints, Abhijit/Madhyahna exceptions, or all tradition-specific do's/don'ts. The interface names these limitations rather than implying they were calculated.

## Shadbala and Ashtakavarga status

Shadbala remains explicitly labelled as a simplified six-component screening estimate, not canonical Shadbala. A canonical replacement requires the full component set (including Sthana subcomponents, complete Kala components, motion/war conventions, and Drik Bala rules) and pinned examples for a declared school/source. A partial rewrite would be more dangerous than a visible limitation.

Ashtakavarga BAV/SAV now uses the contributor-to-target-sign relationship correctly (the prior code incorrectly held the target sign fixed across the twelve output signs). Graha-wise Trikona Shodhana, Ekadhipatya Shodhana and per-graha Shodhya Pinda are now implemented with explicit BPHS/PVR-style assumptions and a standard 337-SAV-total invariant. This is a substantial correction, but the exact Pinda values still need to be compared against a pinned published chart fixture under the same occupied-sign rules and Virgo rasimana multiplier before calling every output canonical.

## Run and deploy

```bash
python -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate  # Windows: .venv\\Scripts\\activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
pytest -q
uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
```

Before production deployment:

1. Back up the current application and data folder.
2. Deploy to staging and run the full test suite, `/health`, `/api/personal/analyze`, `/api/muhurta/search`, and `/api/mundane/analyze` smoke tests.
3. Check that app/static version labels agree with the server version.
4. Run the D9 regression fixture and independent ephemeris comparison for several dates, latitudes, timezones, and dates near midnight.
5. Validate local sunrise/set against a trusted panchanga for the chosen city and selected ayanamsha.
6. Review candidate muhurta output manually for each activity profile before real ceremonial use.
7. Promote only if all staging checks pass; retain the previous ZIP for rollback.

## Known limitations

- A high muhurta score does not mean an event will succeed.
- The present rule weights are transparent engineering choices, not learned or statistically calibrated parameters.
- No guarantee is made for legal, financial, health or safety outcomes; those require domain expertise and practical constraints.
- The complete test suite is necessary for release acceptance; targeted tests alone are insufficient.

# Jyotish Lab 1.0.6 — Security & UI Reliability Build

A local Vedic astrology research and reporting workspace built around Swiss Ephemeris.

## Two independent products in one application

### Personal Astrology

The personal workspace accepts an individual's birth details and produces:

- D1 / Rashi Kundali
- Chandra Lagna and Surya Lagna reference charts
- 16 principal Parashari Vargas with visible charts, including D9 Navamsa and D10 Dashamsha
- Graha matrix: sign, house, degree, dignity, retrograde, combustion
- Planetary aspects
- 27 Nakshatra metadata and interpretation, including Moon Nakshatra and Pada
- Panchanga snapshot: weekday, tithi/paksha, nakshatra, yoga and karana
- Vimshottari Dasha hierarchy through five levels
- Yoga and Dosha screening
- Normalized planet/bhava strength screens
- Jaimini Chara Karakas
- Arudha Padas
- Ashtakavarga matrix
- Sahams
- Dynamic forecasts in days, months or years
- Selectable Parashari, Jaimini, Tajaka/Varshaphala or multi-school synthesis layers
- Shadbala-inspired six-component screening estimate (component formulas are simplified and explicitly labelled approximate)
- BAV/SAV contributor tables with Prastara, graha-wise Trikona and Ekadhipatya Shodhana, per-graha Shodhya Pinda using explicit BPHS/PVR-style assumptions
- Alternative timing systems: Chara, Ashtottari, Yogini, Narayana and Kalachakra research layers
- Tajaka solar returns, Muntha and annual year-lord screens
- Exact planetary ingress/station calendar and Swiss Ephemeris eclipse calendar
- Daily, weekly, monthly or quarterly forecast sampling
- Life-area activation graphs and planning windows
- Detailed PDF with Kundalis, varga atlas, Nakshatra, Dashas, strengths and timing

### Mundane Astrology

The country chart remains the primary collective chart. Optionally, a user can select a saved national leader or enter custom leader birth details; the app evaluates the leader chart separately over the same custom date range and displays date-level corroboration without inventing a combined probability.

The application resolves the country's chart from its national-event corpus and performs:

- Canonical country chart selection
- Alternative national charts
- Country planetary signature
- Same-country historical analogues
- Worldwide historical analogues
- Near-term and long-term transit scans
- Exact ingress, retrograde-station and eclipse calendars
- Expanded dated national/world event corpus with source-quality caveats and date-only/noon proxies identified
- Temporal walk-forward holdout audit with deterministic control dates
- Explicit political event classes including public uprising, leadership transition, government fall and constitutional crisis
- Stock-market and commodity-direction event classes (not empirically validated financial forecasts)
- Dynamic country activation graphs
- Historical-event comparisons attached to future windows

Historical similarity is presented as an astrological research pattern, not causal evidence or a guaranteed event forecast.

### Reference Library

A separate public-figure reference library is included for chart verification. Profiles distinguish verified birth date/place from uncertain birth time. If no reliably verified public birth time is stored, the software uses 12:00 local only as a clearly labelled reference chart rather than pretending it is a timed Kundali.

## Backend diagnostics and privacy

The backend logs request paths, processing stages, counts and durations. Birth name/date/time, birthplace, coordinates, place-search text and chat questions are redacted from application logs. Do not enable verbose request-body logging at a reverse proxy. Unexpected exceptions are recorded server-side while generic 5xx messages are returned to clients.

## Run locally

```bash
python -m venv .venv
.venv\\Scripts\\Activate.ps1       # Windows PowerShell
# source .venv/bin/activate          # macOS/Linux
python -m pip install --upgrade pip
pip install -r requirements.txt
pytest -q
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --reload
```

Open `http://127.0.0.1:8000`.

API docs (development only): `http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/docs`. Set `APP_ENV=production` to disable the interactive docs and OpenAPI schema endpoints.

## Methodology and audit boundary

Astronomical positions use Swiss Ephemeris with Lahiri/Chitra Paksha sidereal settings. Whole-sign houses are used for the main interpretive house framework. Some traditional systems have school-specific variants. Shadbala remains a simplified six-component screening estimate; Ashtakavarga reductions use documented BPHS/PVR-style assumptions and still require pinned independent fixture comparisons; the yoga detector is a rule-screening catalogue, not an exhaustive classical adjudicator.

See `RESEARCH_NOTES.md`, `AUDIT_AND_ASTROLOGICAL_CORRECTNESS_1.0.4.md`, `MUHURTA_AND_VALIDATION_METHOD.md` and `REFERENCE_VALIDATION_MANIFEST.json` for source references, corrected calculation logic, known limitations, the prioritized validation backlog and deployment gate. The test suite validates implementation invariants and reproducibility; it does not prove astrological predictive validity.

This software is interpretive astrology, not scientific prediction, medical diagnosis, financial advice, or a guarantee of future events.

## Jyotish Lab 0.8.7 — research-grade depth release

This release separates Personal Astrology, Mundane Astrology, and the Reference Library into independent workspaces. Personal analysis adds a broad yoga catalogue with fruitification timing screens, expanded dosha screens, numerology, and black/white production UI. Mundane analysis adds a substantially expanded historical event corpus, country-chart resolution, same-country/worldwide analogue matching, near/long forecasts, a retrospective signal-coverage harness, and PDF export.

The astronomical layer uses Swiss Ephemeris sidereal Lahiri calculations. Swiss Ephemeris documents Lahiri as a supported sidereal mode and `swe_houses_ex()` for sidereal house calculations.

The breadth of the feature map is informed by established open-source Jyotish software such as PyJHora and related reference implementations, which document extensive divisional-chart, yoga, dosha, dasha, Ashtakavarga, strength, transit, Jaimini and Tajaka coverage. This project remains a separate implementation and does not claim that every classical school uses identical rules.

### Validation policy

The test suite verifies deterministic calculations, API behavior, PDF generation, and retrospective signal coverage. The interpretation engine is deliberately direct and reproducible: identical inputs produce identical rule output, and difficult signals are not randomized or hidden.


### Deterministic interpretation mode (0.9.0)

The interpretation layer uses a reproducible rule corpus and direct-result rendering. The expanded corpus contains 32,228 explicit combinations covering planetary placements, planet pairs, Dasha pairs, transit contacts, Vargas, Yoga/Dosha life impacts and mundane country forecast domains. Personal forecast rows expose 16 domains; mundane country rows expose 14 national domains. Corpus regeneration is available through `scripts/expand_interpretation_corpus.py`.


## Custom forecast windows (1.0.2)
Both Personal and Mundane workspaces accept an optional start/end date pair. When both dates are selected, the forecast is restricted to that interval; leave both blank to use the horizon controls. Consecutive mundane dates with identical complete outcomes are grouped into a date range. Mundane political event classes include internal unrest, public uprising, leadership transition/removal, government fall and constitutional crisis, with summary, mechanism and corroboration interpretation layers.

## Country + leader forecasts (v1.0.3)

The Mundane workspace can optionally include a leader from the public reference library or manually entered birth data. The country chart remains the primary collective chart; the leader chart is a separate timing lens. If birth time is unknown, a noon reference is clearly labelled and house/Varga conclusions are uncertain. Country and leader timing overlaps are presented as research signals, not a calibrated joint probability. See `LEADER_COUNTRY_SYNTHESIS_AND_DEPLOYMENT.md` for methodology, sources, validation boundaries, and the deployment checklist.


## Personalized Muhurta (1.0.5)

The Personal Astrology workspace includes a personalized Muhurta search for puja, job joining, property purchase/sale, business start, major expense/payment, property registration, travel and education. Set the actual location, timezone and date range (up to 90 days). Supplying birth date/time enables a natal-Moon Tara Bala/Chandra Bala screen. Scores are transparent rule-screen scores, not probabilities or empirically validated outcome forecasts. See `MUHURTA_AND_VALIDATION_METHOD.md` for conventions, validation strategy, deployment steps and limitations.


## Security and privacy (1.0.6)

- Response hardening headers include a restrictive Content Security Policy, frame denial, MIME sniffing protection, a referrer policy and disabled camera/microphone/geolocation permissions. HTML controls no longer use inline event-handler attributes.
- Request bodies are capped at 1 MB, including chunked bodies. Personal forecasts, custom date ranges, library analysis, chat history and historical backtests have explicit input bounds.
- The world-event sync endpoint is disabled unless `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN` is configured. When enabled, the UI prompts for the token and sends it in `X-Admin-Token`; use HTTPS for any non-local deployment.
- Place searches for non-preset locations are sent to the public Nominatim/OpenStreetMap geocoder; avoid searching for sensitive personal addresses.
- AI interpretation is optional. When enabled, the generated chart interpretation payload is sent to the configured `AI_API_URL`; use only a provider you trust. Dynamic text is escaped before PDF markup parsing. Remote provider URLs must use HTTPS; plain HTTP is accepted only for localhost development. Provider error bodies are not exposed to users and responses are capped at 2 MB.
- There is no user-account/authentication or per-user data isolation in this single-user research application. Do not expose it directly to the public internet without adding authentication, TLS termination, reverse-proxy rate limits, secure secret management, and an appropriate data-retention policy. In-memory controls are not a substitute for multi-worker production rate limiting.
- Set `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN` to a long random secret only if you intend to use the browser sync control. Leave it unset to disable that administrative action. `.env` is ignored by Git; never commit real API keys or sync tokens.

See `SECURITY_AUDIT_1.0.6.md` for findings, fixes, tests and residual deployment risks.

FILE: SECURITY_AUDIT_1.0.6.md
================================================================================
# Jyotish Lab 1.0.6 — Security, reliability and UI audit

**Audit date:** 2026-10-21  
**Scope:** FastAPI routes and request schemas, filesystem/subprocess/network operations, logs and error handling, browser rendering/event handlers, forecast resource limits, package/deployment configuration, and interactive smoke tests.

## Executive result

The application had several security and reliability weaknesses appropriate to fix before further use: an unauthenticated endpoint could run the world-event synchronization subprocess; birth details were written to terminal logs; there was no request-body size ceiling; some analysis horizons and backtest parameters allowed excessive work; the UI permitted inline event handlers and its mundane PDF button referenced a missing function; and browser clipboard access could raise unhandled errors. These have been addressed in this build.

This remains a **single-user research application, not a production multi-user service**. It has no account authentication, authorization model for personal charts, or distributed rate limiter. Do not expose it directly to the public internet without the deployment controls listed below.

## Findings and changes

| Area | Finding before patch | Change in 1.0.6 | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Administrative endpoint | `POST /api/mundane/world-events/sync` launched a Python subprocess with no authentication. | Endpoint is disabled unless `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN` is configured; supplied token is checked with constant-time comparison; no subprocess output is returned to clients. UI prompts for token. | Fixed |
| Privacy/logging | Personal calculation and engine logs included name, date/time of birth, birthplace and location coordinates; place searches could be logged. | Sensitive fields are redacted in application phase logs; engine logs no longer print birth details; HTTP logs contain path/status/duration only. | Fixed for application logs |
| Response hardening | No consistent security headers or Content Security Policy. | Added CSP, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, and conservative cache headers. | Fixed |
| Inline event handlers | HTML and generated result markup used `onclick` attributes, which require a weaker CSP. | Replaced inline HTML event handlers with delegated `data-action` / `data-*` dispatch and removed duplicate subtab wiring. `script-src 'self'` is now viable. | Fixed |
| Request size | No body-size cap, including requests without a trustworthy `Content-Length`. | ASGI middleware buffers at most 1 MB and rejects larger request bodies with HTTP 413. | Fixed |
| Unbounded inputs | Library analysis accepted a generic dictionary and directly cast caller-controlled forecast parameters; personal and mundane forecast periods had overly broad unit-specific limits; backtest query bounds were loose. | Added strict Pydantic schemas, extra-field rejection, unit-aware horizon/date-range limits, chat question/history bounds, library search bounds, and bounded backtest query parameters. | Fixed |
| Geocoder | Query length and response size were not bounded; failure messages exposed exception details. | Query limited to 120 characters, URL query encoded, response capped at 512 KB, timeout retained, errors sanitized. Non-preset place searches are sent to Nominatim/OpenStreetMap and should not contain sensitive addresses. | Fixed |
| AI provider transport | Configured AI provider URLs could use non-TLS HTTP and provider errors were returned verbatim; response reads were unbounded. | Remote AI URLs must use HTTPS; HTTP is allowed only for localhost development. Provider response capped at 2 MB and errors sanitized. | Fixed |
| PDF text parsing | User-provided name/place and optional external AI text were inserted into ReportLab `Paragraph` markup without escaping. | Added XML escaping for dynamic Paragraph text, including the chart header, AI output and dynamic interpretation fields. | Fixed |
| Health endpoint | Public health response included internal chat-cache statistics. | Health now returns status, engine and version only. | Fixed |
| Frontend redundancy/reliability | Library and leader selectors could issue redundant initial requests; search fired for every keystroke; clipboard failures could become unhandled promise errors. | Shared initial library promise, debounced/abortable search, one subtab click handler, safe clipboard fallback/error UI. | Fixed |
| Mundane PDF export | UI action was wired to `downloadMundanePdf()` but the function was missing. | Implemented guarded PDF export with response handling, download and URL cleanup. | Fixed |
| Error details | Several generic exception paths returned exception class/message to the caller. | Generic 5xx messages are returned; detailed trace remains server-side. Expected validation errors remain readable. | Fixed for generic failure paths |

## Controls and test coverage

- **102 automated tests passed** with `pytest -q`.
- `python -m py_compile app/*.py scripts/*.py scripts_run_validation.py` passed.
- `node --check static/app.js` passed.
- Live in-process API smoke checks passed for the home page, health, static JavaScript, library, preset geocoder, world-event status, advanced reference endpoints, personalized Muhurta, personal analysis, and PDF generation.
- Security regression checks cover response headers, sync-token denial, body-size enforcement, schema bounds, HTTPS-only remote AI endpoints, public health minimization and inline-handler removal.
- Browser DOM interaction smoke test ran in Chromium with representative live engine output fixtures and mocked network transport: workspace navigation, birth-time picker open/close/apply, demo load, personal analysis render, all 15 personal tabs, AI build/copy/download, chat, personal PDF, Muhurta search, country render, country presets, custom-leader toggle, both historical date-range buttons, mundane backtest, mundane PDF, sync prompt, reference profile selection/category filter, location autocomplete selection and clear-place control. **No page errors or console errors remained.**
- Direct Chromium navigation to the local Uvicorn URL was blocked by this environment's browser policy (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ADMINISTRATOR`), so the DOM interaction test used `page.set_content`; API integration was tested separately through FastAPI `TestClient`.
- `pip check` reports a pre-existing shared-environment conflict: `moviepy 2.2.1` requires `pillow<12`, while the shared environment has `pillow 12.3.0`. Neither package is a direct dependency of this app's declared runtime requirements. A CVE database scanner (`pip-audit`) was not installed in the environment, so this audit does **not** certify all third-party dependency versions as vulnerability-free.

## Residual risks / deployment gate

1. **No user authentication or tenant isolation.** Anyone who can reach the app can submit chart requests and view generated responses from their own request; the app does not store a chart database, but it also has no account boundary. Add authentication and authorization before hosting for multiple users.
2. **No built-in distributed rate limiting.** CPU-heavy astrology and backtest routes should be rate-limited at the reverse proxy or API gateway. In-memory rate limiting would not be reliable across workers/replicas.
3. **TLS is a deployment responsibility.** Terminate HTTPS at a trusted reverse proxy; never send the sync token or AI API key over a public plain-HTTP connection. HSTS should be configured at the HTTPS proxy after verifying the deployment domain.
4. **AI privacy boundary.** If AI credentials are configured, the app sends its generated interpretation payload—including chart-derived data—to the configured AI provider. Use a provider approved for the data you enter. AI is optional and is not called if no key is configured.
5. **Secret handling.** Keep `.env` private and out of source control. Use a secret manager in hosted deployments. Use a long random `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN`; leave it unset when web-triggered sync is unnecessary.
6. **Reverse-proxy limits.** Configure proxy request-size and timeout limits too; app-level limits do not replace perimeter controls.
7. **Dependency freshness.** Run `pip-audit`/OSV or the hosting platform's dependency scanner in CI and review updates on a regular schedule.
8. **No formal penetration test.** This is source-level hardening plus regression/smoke testing, not a third-party penetration test.

## Recommended production proxy controls

- TLS-only ingress; redirect HTTP to HTTPS.
- Authentication in front of all routes (and authorization for admin sync).
- Per-IP and, where applicable, per-account rate limits for `/api/personal/*`, `/api/mundane/*`, `/api/muhurta/search`, and backtest endpoints.
- Request body limit at or below 1 MB, bounded upstream timeouts and concurrency limits.
- Redact request bodies and query strings in proxy logs; do not log birth details or AI prompts.
- Keep `APP_ENV=production` to disable `/api/docs` and `/openapi.json` in a public deployment.
- Store AI and admin tokens in the deployment secret manager, not in image layers or committed files.

## Reproduction commands

```bash
python -m py_compile app/*.py scripts/*.py scripts_run_validation.py
node --check static/app.js
pytest -q
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
```

World-event sync stays disabled until the server environment includes `WORLD_EVENTS_SYNC_TOKEN`. The `.env.example` documents the optional setting.

FILE: VALIDATION_RESULTS_1.0.5.md

# Jyotish Lab 1.0.5 — validation results

## Automated checks

- `pytest -q --disable-warnings --maxfail=1`: **89 passed** (25.4 seconds in the final full run before packaging).
- `node --check static/app.js`: passed.
- `python -m py_compile app/*.py scripts/*.py scripts_run_validation.py`: passed.
- FastAPI `TestClient` smoke test: `/health` returns version `1.0.5`; personalized `/api/muhurta/search` returns 200 for a valid request and 422 for a period exceeding 90 days.
- Muhurta exploratory checks: Kolkata, London, New York and Sydney sunrise/set local dates; weekday Rahu Kalam/Yamaganda/Gulika tables; Panchanga label ranges; deterministic response; optional natal-Moon personalization; 90-day boundary; interval clipping at discrete Panchanga/daylight-segment transitions.
- Muhurta runtime probe: 1-day search ~0.01 s, 14-day ~0.11 s, 90-day ~0.71 s in this execution environment (indicative only).
- Chart regressions: established D9 fixture, all 16 principal Vargas, D30 unequal division boundaries, Vimshottari hierarchy, yoga logic, custom forecast ranges and mundane leader overlays.
- Ashtakavarga regression: BAV/SAV contributor table totals are now invariant at **337** for the selected standard seven-graha contributor tables; Trikona/Ekadhipatya reductions and per-graha Pinda arithmetic are tested.

## Historical mundane replay

The bundled strict chronological replay script completed using the frozen holdout split and did not reach the desired 80% precision target:

| Country / blind period | Threshold selected on development data | Blind predictions | Correct matches | Precision | Recall | F1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Israel, 2024–2026 | 60 | 9 | 3 | 33.3% | 42.9% | 37.5% |
| India, 1990–1999 | 95 | 20 | 1 | 5.0% | 20.0% | 8.0% |

Matched examples recorded by the report:
- Israel: 13 April 2024 Iran attack → `border_clash`.
- Israel: 17 September 2024 Hezbollah pager explosions → `terror_attack`.
- Israel: 8 June 2026 renewed Israel-Iran strikes → `border_clash`.
- India: 3 May 1999 Kargil War begins → `border_clash`.

These are exact-class matches to the bundled corpus, not evidence that the system would have communicated the precise event/date in a real forecast. The low precision, particularly for India, means the event-class engine is not calibrated for dependable operational prediction. The report must not be tuned after seeing holdout results and then still called blind.

## Findings and remaining gates

1. **Ashtakavarga**: corrected a material sign-loop defect; graha-wise reduction logic now uses explicit BPHS/PVR-style conventions. Independent pinned examples are still needed to confirm every Pinda result across schools.
2. **Shadbala**: remains a simplified screening estimate, not a canonical six-bala implementation. The UI and PDF explicitly disclose this.
3. **Muhurta**: calculations and rule screens are tested for numerical/timezone integrity. Activity-specific weighting is a documented tradition-based heuristic, not a statistically calibrated score. It omits some region- and lineage-specific requirements, and a qualified local Panchanga should be consulted for ceremonies with strict local rules.
4. **Predictive validity**: no forecast precision target is claimed. The current mundane blind results are below target; no tuning has been done to force 80%.
5. **Release acceptance**: the complete automated suite passed. Production rollout should still use the documented staging smoke tests, compare a sample of chart and Panchanga outputs against a pinned external reference under matching settings, and retain the prior ZIP for rollback.

def classical_ashtakavarga(planets: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]], lagna_sign: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    signs = {p: sign_index(d["longitude"]) for p, d in planets.items()}
    refs = {p: signs[p] for p in ("Sun","Moon","Mars","Mercury","Jupiter","Venus","Saturn")}
    refs["Lagna"] = lagna_sign
    bav: dict[str, list[int]] = {}
    prastara: dict[str, list[list[int]]] = {}
    for planet, table in BAV_TABLE.items():
        row=[]; matrix=[]
        for s in range(12):
            contributions=[]
            bindu=0
            for ref, ref_sign in refs.items():
                house=((s-ref_sign)%12)+1
                yes=int(house in table[ref])
                contributions.append(yes); bindu+=yes
            row.append(bindu); matrix.append(contributions)
        bav[planet]=row; prastara[planet]=matrix
    sav=[sum(bav[p][s] for p in bav) for s in range(12)]
    # Graha-wise classical-style shodhana: operate on each planet's BAV row,
    # not on the aggregate SAV. The exact school conventions and rasimana
    # multiplier are recorded in the result.
    tri_sets=[(0,4,8),(1,5,9),(2,6,10),(3,7,11)]
    trikona_bav={}; ekadhipatya_bav={}; trikona_steps={}; eka_steps={}
    owner_pairs=[(0,7),(1,6),(2,5),(8,11),(9,10)]
    occupied=set(signs.values()) | {lagna_sign}
    rasi_multipliers=[7,10,8,4,10,6,7,8,9,5,11,12]  # BPHS/PVR-style set; Virgo=6
    graha_multipliers={"Sun":5,"Moon":5,"Mars":8,"Mercury":5,"Jupiter":10,"Venus":7,"Saturn":5}
    per_planet_pinda={}
    for planet,row in bav.items():
        reduced=row[:]; steps=[]
        for trio in tri_sets:
            vals=[reduced[i] for i in trio]
            # Common BPHS/PVR rule: if any sign has zero bindus, no trikona reduction.
            if 0 in vals: steps.append({"signs":[SIGNS[i] for i in trio],"action":"unchanged_zero_present"}); continue
            if vals[0]==vals[1]==vals[2]:
                for i in trio: reduced[i]=0
                steps.append({"signs":[SIGNS[i] for i in trio],"action":"all_zero_equal"})
            else:
                m=min(vals)
                for i in trio: reduced[i]-=m
                steps.append({"signs":[SIGNS[i] for i in trio],"action":"subtract_minimum","amount":m})
        trikona_bav[planet]=reduced
        trikona_steps[planet]=steps
        eka=reduced[:]; est=[]
        for a,b in owner_pairs:
            if eka[a]==0 or eka[b]==0 or (a in occupied and b in occupied):
                est.append({"signs":[SIGNS[a],SIGNS[b],],"action":"unchanged_zero_or_both_occupied"}); continue
            occ_a=a in occupied; occ_b=b in occupied
            if not occ_a and not occ_b:
                if eka[a]==eka[b]: eka[a]=eka[b]=0; action="both_empty_equal_zero"
                else: m=min(eka[a],eka[b]); eka[a]=eka[b]=m; action="both_empty_equalize_to_min"
            elif occ_a:
                if eka[b] <= eka[a]: eka[b]=0; action="empty_b_reduced_to_zero"
                else: eka[b]=eka[a]; action="empty_b_capped_to_occupied"
            else:
                if eka[a] <= eka[b]: eka[a]=0; action="empty_a_reduced_to_zero"
                else: eka[a]=eka[b]; action="empty_a_capped_to_occupied"
            est.append({"signs":[SIGNS[a],SIGNS[b]],"action":action})
        ekadhipatya_bav[planet]=eka; eka_steps[planet]=est
        rp=sum(eka[i]*rasi_multipliers[i] for i in range(12))
        gp=sum(graha_multipliers[ref]*eka[signs[ref]] for ref in graha_multipliers)
        per_planet_pinda[planet]={"rasi_pinda":rp,"graha_pinda":gp,"shodhya_pinda":rp+gp}
    return {"method":"Classical BAV/SAV contributor tables with graha-wise Trikona Shodhana, Ekadhipatya Shodhana and Shodhya Pinda; BPHS/PVR-style assumptions", 
            "bav":bav,"sav":sav,"prastara":prastara,
            "trikona_shodhana":{p:trikona_bav[p] for p in trikona_bav},
            "trikona_steps":trikona_steps,
            "ekadhipatya_shodhana":ekadhipatya_bav,"ekadhipatya_steps":eka_steps,
            "shodhya_pinda":{"rasi_pinda":sum(v['rasi_pinda'] for v in per_planet_pinda.values()),"reduced_pinda":sum(v['shodhya_pinda'] for v in per_planet_pinda.values()),"per_planet":per_planet_pinda},
            "conventions":{"rasimana_multipliers":rasi_multipliers,"grahamana_multipliers":graha_multipliers,"occupied_signs_for_ekadhipatya":[SIGNS[i] for i in sorted(occupied)],"school":"BPHS/PVR-style; source conventions differ, especially Virgo rasimana multiplier"},
            "validation":{"expected_sav_total":337,"actual_sav_total":sum(sav),"sav_total_pass":sum(sav)==337,"rahu_ketu_excluded_from_bav_contributors":True}}



def _saptavarga_score(planet: str, varga_signs: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]]) -> float:
    score=0.0
    for key in ("D1","D2","D3","D7","D9","D12","D30"):
        sign=varga_signs.get(key,{}).get("planets",{}).get(planet,{}).get("sign_index")
        if sign is None: continue
        if sign==EXALTATIONS.get(planet): score += 30
        elif sign==MULATRIKONA.get(planet): score += 22.5
        elif sign in OWN_SIGNS.get(planet,set()): score += 15
        else: score += 3.75
    return score


def shadbala(planets: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]], lagna_sign: int, vargas: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]], birth_local: datetime) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    rows={}
    # Six-fold framework. The implementation is auditable and uses 60-virupa component units.
    for p in ("Sun","Moon","Mars","Mercury","Jupiter","Venus","Saturn"):
        d=planets[p]; lon=d["longitude"]; house=_whole_sign_house(lagna_sign,sign_index(lon))
        ex=EXALTATIONS[p]; dist=angular_distance(lon, ex*0+({"Sun":10,"Moon":33,"Mars":298,"Mercury":165,"Jupiter":95,"Venus":357,"Saturn":200}[p]))
        uccha=max(0.0,60.0*(1-dist/180.0))
        sapt=_saptavarga_score(p,vargas)
        kendra={1:60,4:60,7:60,10:60,2:30,5:30,8:30,11:30}.get(house,15)
        sthana=uccha+sapt+kendra
        strong_house=DIG_STRONG[p]; diff=abs(house-strong_house); diff=min(diff,12-diff)
        dig=max(0.0,60.0*(1-diff/6.0))
        # Kala: day/night + waxing/waning approximation. Moon gets the strongest paksha contribution.
        day=birth_local.hour>=6 and birth_local.hour<18
        kala=30.0 + (15.0 if (p in ("Sun","Jupiter","Venus") and day) or (p in ("Moon","Mars","Saturn") and not day) else 0.0)
        if p=="Moon": kala += 15.0
        chesta=45.0 if d.get("retrograde") else 30.0
        if p in ("Sun","Moon"): chesta=30.0
        nais=NAISARGIKA[p]
        # Drik: signed sum of exact classical aspect relationships, capped.
        drik=0.0
        for q,qdat in planets.items():
            if q==p or q not in ("Sun","Moon","Mars","Mercury","Jupiter","Venus","Saturn"): continue
            dd=angular_distance(lon,qdat["longitude"])
            near=min((0,60,90,120,180),key=lambda x:abs(dd-x)); orb=abs(dd-near)
            if orb<=5:
                benefic=q in ("Jupiter","Venus") or (q=="Mercury" and not qdat.get("combust",False))
                drik += (10-orb*2) if benefic else -(10-orb*2)
        drik=max(-30,min(30,drik))
        total=sthana+dig+kala+chesta+nais+drik
        rupas=total/60
        pct=100*rupas/SHADBALA_MIN_RUPAS[p]
        ishta=math.sqrt(max(0,uccha*min(60,chesta)))
        kashta=math.sqrt(max(0,(60-uccha)*(60-min(60,chesta))))
        rows[p]={"sthana":round(sthana,2),"dig":round(dig,2),"kala":round(kala,2),"cheshta":round(chesta,2),"naisargika":round(nais,2),"drik":round(drik,2),"total_virupas":round(total,2),"rupas":round(rupas,2),"minimum_rupas":SHADBALA_MIN_RUPAS[p],"strength_pct":round(pct,1),"classification":"strong" if pct>=150 else "adequate" if pct>=100 else "below-minimum","ishta_phala":round(ishta,2),"kashta_phala":round(kashta,2)}
    return {"method":"Shadbala-inspired six-component screening estimate; not a complete canonical Shadbala calculation", "planets":rows,"units":"virupas / rupas (approximate component units)","note":"Rahu and Ketu are excluded. Sthana/Kala/Cheshta/Drik component formulas are simplified proxies; do not treat strength_pct or classification as a canonical Shadbala result. Full classical bala requires complete subcomponent calculations and chosen school conventions."}


def _jd_to_dt(jd: float, tz: str = "UTC") -> datetime:
    y,m,d,ut=swe.revjul(jd, swe.GREG_CAL)
    h=int(ut); mins=(ut-h)*60; mi=int(mins); sec=round((mins-mi)*60)
    if sec>=60: sec=0; mi+=1
    if mi>=60: mi=0; h+=1
    dt=datetime(int(y),int(m),int(d),h,mi,min(sec,59),tzinfo=timezone.utc)
    return dt.astimezone(ZoneInfo(tz))


def _bisect_root(fn, lo: float, hi: float, iterations: int=32) -> float:
    flo=fn(lo); fhi=fn(hi)
    for _ in range(iterations):
        mid=(lo+hi)/2; fm=fn(mid)
        if abs(fm)<1e-7: return mid
        if flo*fm<=0: hi=mid; fhi=fm
        else: lo=mid; flo=fm
    return (lo+hi)/2


def exact_planetary_events(start: datetime, end: datetime, tz: str="UTC") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Find sign ingresses and retrograde stations by numerical refinement."""
    start=start.astimezone(timezone.utc); end=end.astimezone(timezone.utc)
    jd0=_time_to_jd(start); jd1=_time_to_jd(end)
    events=[]
    planets=["Mercury","Venus","Mars","Jupiter","Saturn","Rahu"]
    ids={p:{"Mercury":swe.MERCURY,"Venus":swe.VENUS,"Mars":swe.MARS,"Jupiter":swe.JUPITER,"Saturn":swe.SATURN,"Rahu":swe.MEAN_NODE}[p] for p in planets}
    step=1.0
    cur=jd0
    prev={}
    while cur<=jd1:
        for p in planets:
            xx=swe.calc_ut(cur,ids[p],swe.FLG_SWIEPH|swe.FLG_SIDEREAL|swe.FLG_SPEED)[0]
            lon,speed=norm360(xx[0]),xx[3]
            s=sign_index(lon)
            if p in prev:
                pl,ps,p_speed=prev[p]
                if s!=ps:
                    # sign boundary root using shortest angular distance to target boundary
                    target=(s*30)%360
                    def f(j):
                        l=norm360(swe.calc_ut(j,ids[p],swe.FLG_SWIEPH|swe.FLG_SIDEREAL)[0][0])
                        return ((l-target+180)%360)-180
                    root=_bisect_root(f,cur-step,cur)
                    events.append({"type":"ingress","planet":p,"from_sign":SIGNS[ps],"to_sign":SIGNS[s],"datetime":_jd_to_dt(root,tz).isoformat()})
                if (speed<0 and prev[p][2]>=0) or (speed>=0 and prev[p][2]<0):
                    def sf(j):
                        return swe.calc_ut(j,ids[p],swe.FLG_SWIEPH|swe.FLG_SIDEREAL|swe.FLG_SPEED)[0][3]
                    try:
                        root=_bisect_root(sf,cur-step,cur,iterations=40)
                    except Exception:
                        root=cur
                    events.append({"type":"station","planet":p,"station":"retrograde" if speed<0 else "direct","datetime":_jd_to_dt(root,tz).isoformat(),"speed_before":round(prev[p][2],6),"speed_after":round(speed,6)})
            prev[p]=(lon,s,speed)
        cur+=step
    return {"events":events,"method":"Daily ephemeris scan with numerical refinement around sign boundaries and speed sign changes.","precision_note":"Station times are refined to the local numerical root; ingress times are ephemeris-refined but should be checked against the selected ephemeris settings."}


def eclipse_events(start: datetime, end: datetime, tz: str="UTC") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    out=[]; cursor=_time_to_jd(start.astimezone(timezone.utc)); end_jd=_time_to_jd(end.astimezone(timezone.utc))
    while cursor<end_jd:
        flag,t=swe.sol_eclipse_when_glob(cursor,swe.FLG_SWIEPH,0,False); mx=t[0]
        if mx<=cursor or mx>end_jd: break
        out.append({"type":"solar_eclipse","maximum":_jd_to_dt(mx,tz).isoformat(),"flags":int(flag)})
        cursor=mx+1
    cursor=_time_to_jd(start.astimezone(timezone.utc))
    while cursor<end_jd:
        flag,t=swe.lun_eclipse_when(cursor,swe.FLG_SWIEPH,0,False); mx=t[0]
        if mx<=cursor or mx>end_jd: break
        out.append({"type":"lunar_eclipse","maximum":_jd_to_dt(mx,tz).isoformat(),"flags":int(flag)})
        cursor=mx+1
    return {"events":sorted(out,key=lambda x:x["maximum"]),"method":"Swiss Ephemeris global eclipse search.","note":"Global eclipse occurrence; local visibility requires a separate topocentric/local-visibility calculation."}


def _solar_return_jd(natal_sun: float, year: int) -> float:
    # Search around the birthday in UTC and refine the first Sun longitude crossing.
    approx=datetime(year,1,1,tzinfo=timezone.utc)
    start=_time_to_jd(approx); prev=start
    def angle(j):
        return ((norm360(swe.calc_ut(j,swe.SUN,swe.FLG_SWIEPH|swe.FLG_SIDEREAL)[0][0])-natal_sun+180)%360)-180
    pv=angle(prev)
    for i in range(1,370):
        cur=start+i
        cv=angle(cur)
        if pv*cv<=0:
            return _bisect_root(angle,prev,cur)
        prev=cur; pv=cv
    return start


def tajaka_year(natal: Mapping[str, Any], birth: datetime, year: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    jd=_solar_return_jd(natal["planets"]["Sun"]["longitude"],year)
    dt=_jd_to_dt(jd,birth.tzinfo.key if getattr(birth.tzinfo,"key",None) else "UTC")
    pos=_calc_planets(jd); flags=swe.FLG_SWIEPH|swe.FLG_SIDEREAL
    cusps,ascmc=swe.houses_ex(jd,natal["meta"]["latitude"],natal["meta"]["longitude"],b"W",flags)
    asc=norm360(ascmc[0]); muntha=norm360(natal["lagna_longitude"]+30*((year-birth.year)%12))
    year_lord=list(("Sun","Moon","Mars","Mercury","Jupiter","Venus","Saturn"))[((year-birth.year)%7)]
    return {"year":year,"solar_return":dt.isoformat(),"lagna":asc,"lagna_sign":SIGNS[sign_index(asc)],"muntha":muntha,"muntha_sign":SIGNS[sign_index(muntha)],"year_lord":year_lord,"planets":{p:{"longitude":d["longitude"],"sign":SIGNS[sign_index(d["longitude"])]} for p,d in pos.items()},"sahams":{},"method":"Tajaka annual solar-return screen; Muntha/year-lord timing is school-dependent."}


def chara_dasha(lagna_sign: int, start: datetime, years: int=12) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    # Jaimini Chara Dasha is sign-based and has multiple direction rules. This implementation exposes a transparent 12-sign annual cycle.
    signs=list(range(12)); nodes=[]; cur=start
    for i in range(years):
        s=(lagna_sign+i)%12
        end=cur+timedelta(days=365.2425)
        nodes.append({"sign":SIGNS[s],"sign_index":s,"start":cur.isoformat(),"end":end.isoformat(),"years":1})
        cur=end
    return {"system":"Chara Dasha","periods":nodes,"method":"Jaimini sign-dasha research mode using one-year sign periods from Lagna; advanced direction/antardasha variants are selectable in future rule packs."}


def ashtottari_dasha(moon_lon: float, start: datetime, years: int=8) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    order=[("Sun",6), ("Moon",15), ("Mars",8), ("Mercury",17), ("Saturn",10), ("Jupiter",19), ("Rahu",12), ("Venus",21)]
    total=108; span=360/27; idx=int(moon_lon//span)%8; cur=start; out=[]
    for i in range(8):
        lord,dur=order[(idx+i)%8]; end=cur+timedelta(days=365.2425*dur)
        out.append({"lord":lord,"start":cur.isoformat(),"end":end.isoformat(),"years":dur}); cur=end
        if cur>=start+timedelta(days=365.2425*years): break
    return {"system":"Ashtottari Dasha","cycle_years":total,"periods":out,"applicability":"Conditional dasha; chart-school rules should be checked before using it as a primary timing system."}


def yogini_dasha(start: datetime, years:int=8) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    order=[("Mangala",1),("Pingala",2),("Dhanya",3),("Bhramari",4),("Bhadrika",5),("Ulka",6),("Siddha",7),("Sankata",8)]
    cur=start; out=[]
    for name,dur in order*4:
        end=cur+timedelta(days=365.2425*dur); out.append({"lord":name,"years":dur,"start":cur.isoformat(),"end":end.isoformat()}); cur=end
        if (cur-start).days>=365.2425*years: break
    return {"system":"Yogini Dasha","cycle_years":36,"periods":out}


def narayana_dasha(lagna_sign:int,start:datetime,years:int=12)->Dict[str,Any]:
    cur=start; out=[]
    for i in range(years):
        s=(lagna_sign+i)%12; end=cur+timedelta(days=365.2425); out.append({"sign":SIGNS[s],"sign_index":s,"start":cur.isoformat(),"end":end.isoformat(),"years":1}); cur=end
    return {"system":"Narayana Dasha","periods":out,"method":"Sign-based annual research mode; Narayana direction and exceptions vary by lineage."}


def kalachakra_dasha(moon_lon:float,start:datetime,years:int=8)->Dict[str,Any]:
    # The full Kalachakra mapping is highly lineage-specific; expose a deterministic research sequence keyed to Moon nakshatra.
    idx=int(moon_lon/(360/27)); base=[0,4,8,3,7,11,2,6,10,1,5,9]
    cur=start; out=[]
    for i in range(years):
        s=base[(idx+i)%12]; end=cur+timedelta(days=365.2425); out.append({"sign":SIGNS[s],"sign_index":s,"start":cur.isoformat(),"end":end.isoformat(),"years":1}); cur=end
    return {"system":"Kalachakra Dasha","periods":out,"method":"Research-mode sign sequence keyed to lunar nakshatra; full traditional gati/pada logic is lineage-specific."}


def sudarshana_chakra(natal: Mapping[str,Any], transits: Sequence[Mapping[str,Any]]) -> Dict[str,Any]:
    lag=natal["lagna_sign_index"]; moon=sign_index(natal["planets"]["Moon"]["longitude"]); sun=sign_index(natal["planets"]["Sun"]["longitude"])
    rows=[]
    for tr in transits:
        scores={"Lagna":0,"Moon":0,"Sun":0}
        for base,label in ((lag,"Lagna"),(moon,"Moon"),(sun,"Sun")):
            for p in ("Jupiter","Saturn"):
                h=_whole_sign_house(base,sign_index(tr["planets"][p]))
                if h in (1,5,9,10,11): scores[label]+=20
                elif h in (6,8,12): scores[label]-=10
        rows.append({"date":tr["date"],**scores,"composite":round(sum(scores.values())/3,1)})
    return {"method":"Sudarshana Chakra three-reference screen (Lagna, Moon, Sun)","rows":rows}


def advanced_timing(natal: Mapping[str,Any], birth: datetime, transits: Sequence[Mapping[str,Any]], school:str="synthesis") -> Dict[str,Any]:
    school=school if school in SCHOOL_PROFILES else "synthesis"
    start=datetime.fromisoformat(transits[0]["date"]).replace(tzinfo=birth.tzinfo) if transits else birth
    end=datetime.fromisoformat(transits[-1]["date"]).replace(tzinfo=birth.tzinfo) if transits else birth+timedelta(days=365)
    return {"school":school,"school_profile":SCHOOL_PROFILES[school],
            "chara_dasha":chara_dasha(natal["lagna_sign_index"],birth,max(12,((end-birth).days//365)+1)),
            "ashtottari":ashtottari_dasha(natal["planets"]["Moon"]["longitude"],birth,max(8,((end-birth).days//365)+1)),
            "yogini":yogini_dasha(birth,max(8,((end-birth).days//365)+1)),
            "narayana":narayana_dasha(natal["lagna_sign_index"],birth,max(12,((end-birth).days//365)+1)),
            "kalachakra":kalachakra_dasha(natal["planets"]["Moon"]["longitude"],birth,max(8,((end-birth).days//365)+1)),
            "sudarshana":sudarshana_chakra(natal,transits)}

FILE: app/ai_interpreter.py
